tbmm-2016-11-02sit02spe0176par0125	Mr. President, I would like to present our views as the National Movement Party Group on the bill of the Specialist Act, 388 rows under discussion. On this occasion, I salute you all. There has been no study of this bill, while it has been mandatory to analyse the regulatory effect on each bill in procedures and fundamentally on preparation. This makes it difficult to study the bill and assess the effects of the arrangements brought in. This bill includes general regulations concerning expertise, the basic principles of expertise, the qualifications of experts, selection and auditing, and some regulations concerning the Forensic Medicine Institution, and the compliance of the provisions related to the expertise in other laws. The institutions that have been given jurisdiction by the law are excluded from the scope. It is a fact that the judicial system still does not function well in Turkey. Therefore, I must first say that we feel it is necessary for experts to be organized and transformed into a structure that will contribute to the establishment of justice, especially to their selection and control. As a matter of fact, in our election statements as the National Movement Party, the sentence in the criminal, administrative and military judiciary, the selection and control of experts in the legal and administrative process will be restored to a structure that will contribute to the fulfillment of justice. It has been pointed out both the shortcomings in this area and the solution. However, the bill is not seen to eliminate problems in this sense undisputedly; its regulations provide question marks on how to address the current problems, a structure that provides a basis for the emergence of more troubled situations than the present. Arrangements from the bill may be harmful in many issues related to tasks, organizational and responsibilities, and functioning from the boards established. Dear lawmakers, at the top of issues that are important in the capacity of an expert witness is the objective study and presentation of technical issues the court cannot resolve with the knowledge of general law. As a matter of fact, Article 3 of the bill was stated in Article (1) that its expert will carry out its duties independently, objectively and objectively under the rules of honesty. Apart from the ruling, the bill did not include any explanation of the ethical principles that experts should be subject to. At this point, it is considered that it would be wise to place a verdict in the bill, at least for reasons such as the explanation of the case file, to prevent the prosecution from contacting experts. Other than that, it is beneficial for experts to be judged that they can demand the information and documents they need directly from the court. The expert agency is a co-host and proprietary entity in which courts have applied to access special and technical information, which ensures that the justice mechanism will be restored to a modern, scientific structure in the facility of justice, which compares the facts to scientific facts and works as quickly as possible. However, the most important problem with the enforcement agency is that it is being used outside the scope of the judicial system. In fact, courts have observed that access to legal information rather than specific and technical information has been applied to the expert agency. Without a doubt, the use of the expert opinion agency without purpose is based on important areas of trouble already facing the Turkish justice system. Students who have graduated from universities' four-year-old law schools without a comprehensive education in any field and with just general knowledge of law are again accepted as a judge, as a candidate for prosecutors, as a result of the vocational competency exam and interview phases where general legal knowledge has been tested. The candidates are starting to exercise their rule of law by experiencing setbacks, even in the current general legal knowledge, not only in certain areas after a short period of public training, which is extremely inefficient. Such inadequate training and implementation infrastructure, individuals who have been introduced with an extensive and demanding authority to distribute justice at a very young age, have been forced to address their professional inadequacies and inefficiency at the beginning of their careers, with the skill of the expert agency, which they have been forced to engage in, and mainly, in legal matters. In addition, as a criminal judge in our legislation for years and years, given that there are no obstacles to the court of law and the appointment of a similar legal judge to the criminal court, the expert's institution is not only for judges at the head of the profession but also for senior judges. In addition to all of this, the lack of vocational education, the number of judges capable of following current legal developments, the fact that promotional system criteria do not support the professional development of judges and prosecutors, as well as the fact that the judge prosecutor's deficit has caused the file density, suggests that the obligation to the expert institution will continue in the near future. The basis of the problems is a large number of problems in law schools, which extend from the method of adoption in the profession of prosecution to vocational education, from the provision of judicial security to the process of promotion, which ultimately compels the functioning of the mechanism of justice even in legal matters to the expert institution. Trying to organise the expertise will be incomplete without resolving the main problem areas, which are at the root of difficulties related to the expertise institution. In other words, it is daydreaming to expect the problems of the expert institution to be solved by the institutional infrastructure and the control mechanism, without examining why expertise has become a problem in our country. Although the bill will contribute to solving some problems, it is quite wrong to approach it as if it will solve all the problems of the justice system. Because being an expert is the result of problems, not the cause of them. Moreover, the current setbacks regarding the authority are only one of the results caused by the issues. Dear lawmakers, in the first article of the bill, it would be appropriate to place all kinds of judiciary on the basis of the Constitution, Court, discrepancy and election, even though it is stated that the law will cover the judicial, administrative and military judiciary. The 2nd article contains definitions. In this article, it is estimated that private legal persons may also be experts. In the definition of expertise, the involvement of private legal persons may lead to commercialisation of the expert opinion and a function outside the purview of the law by means of commercial corporations for this purpose. Still, the complainant aspects of the agency's expertise will show a more severe negativity with this arrangement. While informing the Justice Commission on the annual number of files the experts have looked at, Mr. Justice Minister said there were experts seen to have looked at 3,797 cases a year, it was impossible to accept that they were making a healthy expert opinion. However, in the case of such legislation, the structure will be institutionalized through corporations, and the problem will grow and deepen. The ruling on the expert opinion of private legal persons is one of the most critical points of this bill that should be addressed and corrected in our view. It is possible, as it is known, to bring together experts from different fields by the judge's discretion, if there are situations in which more than one technical person should be applied for because of technical knowledge and more than one technical person should decide together. Hence, in some cases, an institutional organization may need it. The reason for it is not true. The view that this application can still be carried out under the Penal Code will be considered an opportunity to review the current situation, as there is no justification for this arrangement. The admission of private legal persons as experts will prepare the basis for the creation of private institutions to be experts, for the formation of this business with commercial understanding, and for the development of non-profit developments. The actively inserting of private legal persons into the expertty system will deepen this distortion, with which companies will be created, the system will be commercialized, the allegations of bribery and corruption will be widespread, and judicial decisions will be debated, even if they are correct, and their credibility and credibility will always be questioned. Therefore, we feel it is imperative that the bill be removed from the bill, which allows private legal persons to be identified as experts. Article 3 has to be explained whether the basic principles are in order and the issues that are in place here are the rules or the exceptions. If there are exceptions, it is necessary to be explicitly included in the bill and to support the practicality of the principles with appropriate conditions, and the requirements for the obligations specified must be made clear in the progressing clauses. Otherwise, the legal responsibility and responsibility of multiple legislation can be mixed together. While it is indicated that it will not be able to apply to an expert on issues that are possible to be resolved with legal information, it is also considered that the application path to the expert may be needed in some cases, in the case of necessity and necessity, by defining the framework here. In Articles 4 and 5, the duties of the Board and the Council are organised. The board, which will give a permanent consultation as a council within the ministry, is important in terms of accession of the parties, but is seen as a structure that will remain under the shadow of its structure and as part of its duties. For this reason, there is no functional property of the Precognitive Advisory Board. On the other hand, the board has a large membership structure, and the fact that it will be held only twice a year will reduce its contribution to the Council of Counsellors' policy creation. A narrower-wide but more active structure can contribute actively and efficiently to the policy creation of the relevant agency. The framework of those attending the board is unclear by what criteria are based on. In this case, the question of why some NGOs are not involved remains unanswered. It is also seen that their duties are parallel to the duties of the agency's office, which is established with Article 6. At the same time, the council is envisioned as neutral, while the majority of its president and board consists of ministry officials. Serious hesitations about whether members of the ministry will act in accordance with the principle of neutrality will lead to the lack of expected contribution. The additional report is needed. No one is satisfied with the first report. The second report is being filed as a result of objections. And when the second report comes in, the third report goes back to the third expert because it contradicts the initial report. In the face of an expert opinion that has reached three different conclusions, the judge either decides by third or forwards it to the fourth expert to resolve the contradictions. Although the application is not mandatory, most of the time there is an arbitration board, but most of the time, some members of the board's contribution to the final report remains a signature on paper. In their reports, experts have a negative effect on the trial. With the law, certain regulations must be made to prevent this. The opinions of the parties must be taken to determine the questions directed to the expert. This should be added to the bill. The bill does not include executive offices, even though the institutions that will be appointed as experts are considered courts and republican prosecutors. This is a serious flaw. The bill does not include a satisfactory arrangement for the criminal responsibility of its expert. The bill included institutions such as TÜBITAK, Forensic Medicine Agency, but covered areas of judicial, administrative and military judiciary. The bill creates a new board of directors under the name "Information Oversight Board." The task of this Council can be carried out by other agencies still existing in the Ministry of Justice, while a new board is also being formed. Being head of the Supreme Council of the Undersecretary of Justice, which depends on the government and, of course, may, indirectly, damage the judiciary's independence and neutrality and damage the trust in the judiciary. That's why it would be right for the board to be elected president of the Supreme Court. The board also states that a member of the Turkish Bar Association's Governing Body will be selected from the lawyers. However, no criteria have been set to determine the competence of the counsel elected to the board. The same is true of the Association of OSK, Turkish Engineers and Architectures, Turkey's Free Accountant Financial Advisors and the Association of Oathed Financial Advisors. In other words, the councils are established in the district courts, as well as in the region's jurisdictional courts. These boards are like the Supreme Council of Experts. Turkey's Bar Association is also replaced by bars in the provinces of the district courthouses. The negatives mentioned above also apply to these boards. The term for teaching members who serve in the field of law for the faculty elected by the Supreme Kurula YÖK is not required for the district boards in cases where the council will serve without a law school at the universities in the region. Thus, the way for non-lawless lecturers to become members of the board is opened. The bill allows private legal persons to be experts. In this way, commercial companies established to be experts will be established and the expert agency will become a commercial front door. The obligations available for experts, such as swearing, keeping secrets, faithfully and carefully carry out their duties, can only apply to real people. It is not part of the bill to provide these issues for corporate people. Again, an expert is a public duty. I'd like to point out that we don't think it's right to have a public duty to judge, especially as an expert witness, carried out by private legal people. Again, we believe that when it comes to the legal responsibility of the expert expert, there will be serious problems with the responsibility of private legal persons. This arrangement is not right. This must be avoided. Ladies and gentlemen of the law, of course, it's a matter of importance, a bleeding wound. We find the government's bill to be a positive first step, but in time, we feel that its shortcomings need to be corrected. I hereby salute your high council. Thank you.
